### U.7 Audit artifacts, reconstruction limits and unresolved labels

The self-contained semantic/profile evidence kit retains `analysis/CANDIDATE_ADJUDICATION.json` and `.csv` (110 entries), `GENERATION_ADJUDICATION.json` and `.csv` (390 mapped records), `SEMANTIC_REVIEW_SUMMARY.json`, `DOWNSTREAM_IMMEDIATE_23_CELLS.json`, `CLAIM_VERDICTS.json`, `INSPECTION_COVERAGE.json`, `evidence/AUTHENTIC_BASELINE_CONTEXTS.json`, `NATIVE_CANDIDATE_REPLAY.json`, `FROZEN_SUFFIX_REPLAY.json`, `TIMESTAMP_REPRESENTATION_DIAGNOSTIC.json` and full candidate outputs. Its offline recheck command is packaged as `RECHECK_OFFLINE.sh`; the completed fresh-copy offline recheck succeeded. Those names refer to actual supplied analysis assets, not a new remote launch instruction.

The native CPU replay uses AgentDojo 0.1.35 on the analysis environment and actual simulated tools. It is not a reconstruction of the original serving hardware or fresh generation. The observer and native backend are trusted for their stated finite semantics. Nine candidate entries remain underspecified; publication claims that depend on their permission require independent contract/identity adjudication. No new contract is invented to clear an original anchor. No effect versus unresolved effect remains distinct.

The 60 tool-error generations include fifty timestamp-format errors, five missing-Slack-argument errors and five failed-contact lookups. Fifty-five are entirely without mutation; five partially commit an email before calendar failure and are not classified as no-effect. A promise without a call is not refusal or successful execution. These are overlapping diagnostics, not additive attack populations.

The corrected-context certificate preserves all raw-log and prefix hashes and records the 15 transformed-packet error. Old packets remain available as erroneous provenance, not validated independent annotations. The deterministic original suffix is never described as self-generated recovery.


## Appendix V. Release-source identity, effective profiles and local consequences

**Evidence class:** completed retained-source inspection, static fake-client request capture, finite backend certificates and inspection of existing R156 traces. No new model/API calls were made. This appendix distinguishes release code from a complete deployment and the project's interventions. It does not assert freshly reproduced live driver parity.

### V.1. Published profile versus released code versus our study

The [paper](https://www.usenix.org/system/files/usenixsecurity26-he-yu.pdf) describes a principal auxiliary model and decoding setup; its [public artifact](https://zenodo.org/records/20308739) also supplies configurable local drivers. Those are separate reproducibility targets. The following concrete implementation facts come from the retained release files, checked against the published ZIP identity, and our actual archived run source and payloads.

The local `usenix-artifacts.zip` has SHA-256 `81c6d58fdd09c8af217e59dc752dc032dab32c29e204dd7f9d936592174bbf1a`. Its MD5 matches the public record's `e8cf06c6c75abe4d3eb89412842b9cbf`. The core guard has SHA-256 `6d28e2208efbd521bf3f2e90c553e57b11c786e65564eababacb8cdf4f8050d8`. Core and driver members equal their retained extracted copies.

| Dimension | Author release | Actual study / consequence |
|---|---|---|
| Local auxiliaries | Explicitly documented and configurable | All-local is not automatically unsupported; exact behavior still depends on driver/profile |
| Auxiliary decoding | Driver constructs temperature 0.2 and top-p 0.9 for local and OpenAI auxiliary paths | Study sends temperature 0, usually without explicit top-p; genuine generation-profile difference |
| Judge tool information | OpenAI driver attaches runtime tools; local driver injects function schemas into its system prompt | Study judge requests omit these tool schemas; source prompt equality does not establish effective-input equality |
| Actor/reference transport | Release has both native OpenAI-compatible and custom local function-tag paths | Study uses Qwen native calls in some panels and Gemma JSON action envelopes; these are different interfaces |
| Local function-tag parser | Parses the first function block; accompanying prompt requests one-at-a-time calls | Cannot assume the same batch exposure as the study's multi-call JSON/native interfaces; native release path is a separate supported target |
| Judge log probabilities | OpenAI driver forwards them; local driver does not | Study usually omits them; existing fixed-path probes did not change their tested admission vectors, so this alone does not explain those outcomes |
| Original source allocation | Per-call exact/fuzzy admission against the full same-function reference set | Preserved in closest-source email arms; PAIR/capacity and default-key normalization are authored interventions |
| Empty-output routing | Release default skips the following audit after qualifying empty outputs | Preserved in closest-source email arms; continuous-review adaptations change it |
| Feedback identity | Original maps receipts/reasons by call signature | Occurrence-feedback variants change this mapping; source-original feedback arms keep it |
| Tools and tasks | Stock benchmark paths plus explicit release task exclusions | Custom payment backend is authored; native email backend is retained, while task, transport and wrapper remain adapted |
| Main iteration limit | Source constructor default 15 | Study uses 12 and additional request/round caps; censoring must remain visible |

Three replay roots—Slack 18, Slack 19 and workspace 13, covering four decision checkpoints—also occur in the release benchmark's exclusion list. They can still inform our own fidelity study but cannot be silently counted as supported target-benchmark failures. Dataset and benchmark-version differences must be retained.

A native backend and a native model tool-calling transport are distinct properties. For example, the closest-source email panel uses a native Inbox backend, but Qwen and Gemma do not use identical transports.

### V.2. Offline request capture: a concrete missing control

The offline audit invoked the retained release auxiliary drivers with a fake client returning a static response. This captures their exact request construction without any model/API call.

- The OpenAI-compatible driver includes top-level runtime tool definitions, temperature 0.2, top-p 0.9 and forwarded logprob parameters.
- The custom local driver injects available function schemas into the system prompt, uses temperature 0.2/top-p 0.9, and does not forward logprobs.
- Our archived study policy supplies neither representation of runtime tools to the judge and ordinarily uses temperature 0.

This is an identified **input/profile difference**, not evidence that adding schemas will repair security. Its causal influence on non-exact fuzzy decisions is unmeasured. Future faithful-baseline code must preserve a selected release driver's actual payload, not merely copy the fuzzy prompt.

For exact-signature decisions the judge is not called. Judge-schema or judge-temperature differences are therefore inactive at that local admission decision. They may still affect upstream attenuation/reference generation and the frequency of reaching it. A configuration difference is not equally fatal to every claim.

### V.3. What genuinely belongs to the original source

Eight R156 email arms retain original allocation and original feedback. Their generated `_fuzzy_survive`, `_attenuate_observation` and `_call_signature` methods have identical ASTs to the pinned release. The surrounding hooks and model/backend profile still differ.

Within those eight selected arms, four are clean and four hostile. Three hostile arms commit forbidden effects under explicit one-message contracts. This is a small descriptive set, not a published-profile attack rate.

| Selected source-original arm | Recorded consequence | Local mechanism attribution |
|---|---|---|
| Qwen, hostile before-effect notice, E4b_079 | Two actor sends, two reference sends; both exact and admitted; count exceeded | Reference itself supports the excessive batch. One-use-per-reference allocation would not contain two references |
| Gemma, hostile after-effect notice, E4b_109 | Fresh exact support for another send after one was already committed; count exceeded | Reviewed action; current reference agreement does not enforce remaining one-message permission |
| Gemma, hostile before-effect notice, E4b_099 | Two actor sends, one reference; fuzzy admission; count and parameter predicates violated | Source fuzzy rule participates, but study's judge input/profile differs from release drivers |
| Qwen, hostile after-effect notice, E4b_089 | Empty reference; proposed additional send blocked; contract preserved | Important protective outcome; rejects “reference always contaminated” |

The two exact-match cases are not parser-fallback demonstrations and do not need a fuzzy judge decision. The one fuzzy case must retain its profile qualification. Clean controls and the protected hostile after-effect arm remain part of the explanation.

The review-continuity defect also belongs to source routing under specified transitions. Its component behavior is not invented by the occurrence wrapper. Autonomous reachability, threat model, complete patched-workflow utility and prevalence remain separate questions.

### V.4. What belongs to our interventions

Default-key normalization, singleton pairwise judging, injective occurrence allocation, continuous review, occurrence-specific feedback and trusted precommit enforcement are interventions supplied by this project. Their outcomes must not all be attributed to the published defense.

In particular, SOURCE and PAIR use different contextual predicates and are not globally nested. A17's SOURCE-to-PAIR reversal does not establish that a uniformly stricter original guard is less safe. L3's fixed-edge capacity comparison is a better scoped restriction test.

A-P10 remains a genuine clean authorization failure under the tested adapter and a selected closed-loop admission counterexample. It is not an attacker-induced exploit by itself. Service interruption reproducing unsafe recovery remains a strong ordinary-recovery explanation. E5's zero violations in new structures remains negative transfer evidence.

### V.5. Remaining-state and default-key certificates

The pinned source exact-match rule returns true for an identical candidate/reference both before and after a controlled native bill transfer. Actual remaining permission differs. This is an offline conditional demonstration that exact behavioral agreement alone does not consult remaining permission. It is not an autonomous attack, a prevalence result, or a novel authorization theorem.

Omitted and explicitly empty payment keys have identical future state/results across six controlled retry continuations in the archived authored backend, including registered retry, conflicting registered identity and fresh identity. This strengthens the A10 representation-equivalence premise for that backend. It does not generalize to every payment API or create a new idempotency defense.

The key distinction is now precise: an irrelevant representation difference can change a model comparison even when the tested backend future semantics are equal; a case change in a native principal identifier can instead change actual semantics. Canonicalization must follow the tool, not a blanket rule that formatting differences are harmless.

### V.6. Claim-by-claim verdict

| Claim | Verdict | Reason and permitted statement |
|---|---|---|
| Controlled source relocation changes the tested scoring signal | **Established, scoped** | Original matched results survive; external facts and permission remain distinct constructs |
| Corrected replay baseline meets its qualification | **Unsupported / failed** | Original declared gates remain failed for every model |
| Replay fidelity misses demonstrate unauthorized behavior | **Unsupported as a general interpretation** | Lawful alternatives, operational failures and underspecified cases dominate this adjudication |
| Downstream pass proves autonomous safe recovery | **Unsupported** | Original future and final text are imposed; one suffix duplicates a completed reply and another conceals a principal mismatch |
| The reference can agree with an excessive action | **Established in explicit-contract cases** | Later committed-effect evidence, including two exact source-original local admissions |
| Shared hostile content is the universal cause | **Weakened** | Co-exposure, task/profile changes and protective references; causal contribution remains conditional |
| Every source finding is an adapter artifact | **Unsupported** | Core exact admission and routing behavior are source properties; local consequences exist under declared profiles |
| Full published configuration was reproduced | **Unsupported** | Effective auxiliary inputs, decoding, transports, task/version and hooks differ |
| All-local deployment is inherently unsupported | **Unsupported** | The release explicitly offers local auxiliary configuration |
| Judge-input differences explain every violation | **Unsupported** | Exact-match violations bypass the judge; upstream generation effects remain possible |
| Empty versus omitted key is semantically different in A10 backend | **Weakened / contradicted within tested backend** | Source default and six future-continuation tests agree on effect state; comparison sensitivity remains real |
| Source-to-PAIR is a uniformly stricter defense | **Unsupported** | Changes a contextual predicate; use a fixed edge table for an inclusion claim |
| Security blocking uniquely causes unsafe recovery | **Weakened** | Actual service control and older undefended retry evidence supply ordinary-recovery paths |
| Trusted precommit checks contain the selected excessive effects | **Established, scoped** | Three actual denials in R156; usefulness depends on supplied contract and lawful completion |
| New-structure generalization is established | **Unsupported** | E5 has zero violations/24; selected examples do not supply broad transfer |
| A distinctive S&P-level mechanism/novelty is established | **Unresolved** | Source behavior and explicit consequences are real; architecture-wide interpretation and closest-work novelty remain open |


### V.7 Source and request certificate locators

The evidence kit contains `source_profiles/RELEASE_AUX_REQUEST_CAPTURE.json`, `EXACT_MATCH_STATE_ALIAS_CERTIFICATE.json`, `DEFAULT_RETRY_SEMANTICS_CERTIFICATE.json`, `ORIGINAL_FEEDBACK_SOURCE_CASES.json`, `CLOSEST_SOURCE_PROFILE_CERTIFICATE.json`, `RECHECKED_SOURCE_SLOTS.json`, `SOURCE_AND_RUNTIME_IDENTITY.json`, and `analysis/SOURCE_PROFILE_MATRIX.json`/`.csv`. Retain fake-client fixtures and all actual archived payloads alongside these certificates. Request capture establishes what the tested constructor emitted, not what a live model would decide.

The public release identity is associated with the retained Zenodo record `20308739`. Actual source methods and driver files were compared locally in the semantic/profile audit. Public listing identity, a source checksum, AST equivalence, effective-payload parity, native effect semantics, autonomous reachability and end-to-end benchmark fidelity are different levels of evidence. None upgrades automatically into the others.

The historical replay roots Slack 18, Slack 19 and workspace 13 span four decision checkpoints listed in the release exclusion table. Keep them in the author's own fidelity corpus, but do not silently label their outcomes failures on an included release benchmark population.

### V.8 Minimum implementation delta register

Before the next study, fill actual values for source ZIP/core/driver identities; selected supported entry point; dependency versions; actor/reference/attenuator/judge identities; messages and schemas; temperature/top-p/seed/stop/logprobs; thinking/template controls; parsing and multi-call behavior; source exclusions; routing/skip flags; original versus occurrence-based feedback; iteration/request/token bounds; native tool and receipt semantics; observation and checkpoint restoration. Give each deviation a reason and affected claim.

If the chosen original feedback path cannot represent some lawful repeated-signature workflow reliably, preserve that result and its effects. Do not repair it inside RELEASE and still call it original. A separately named occurrence-feedback arm requires a prospective allocation and is outside the current 36-workflow primary matrix.

The source exact-match state-alias certificate holds candidate/reference fixed across two controlled actual permission states. It diagnoses that local input/branch, not an information-theoretic impossibility for an upstream history-sensitive reference or a downstream gate. The six default-key continuation fixtures certify only the tested authored backend and continuations; they do not justify global textual canonicalization.


## Appendix W. Integrated scientific diagnosis, adversarial tests and research decision

### W.1 What survives the evidence-first reconstruction

The project contains good science from the author's original unpublished work and additional consequential development observations. Its strength is not a universal guard failure. It is the ability to separate what a source influences, what a configured reference regenerates, which proposals are admitted, what effects actually commit and which parts of the legitimate task remain unfinished.

The original source method and corrected score/threshold findings remain valid at their stated scope. The original live selected attack comparison is protective. Later adapted payment and email runs show explicit-contract violations and containment, and selected clean payment checkpoints show that changing admission can alter the committed prefix and fresh continuation. Retrospective native replay now shows why literal action divergence and downstream utility cannot be used as permission truth. No one of these observations supplies the missing external novelty or full released-profile claim.

The central unresolved question is:

> Under a supported reference-based execution profile, which authorization-relevant distinctions are preserved or lost between the trusted contract, generated behavioral reference, admitted operation and committed effect prefix—and can those conditions predict both a forbidden effect and a protected neighboring workflow?

This is not a claim that all those losses occur or share one cause. It is a question that can lose to ordinary task failure, a configuration difference, conventional missing enforcement or a protected release profile. A paper about a specific serious implementation boundary can still be valuable; an architecture-wide label is not required and is not earned by these data alone.

### W.2 Evidence and identifiability stress test

| Claim/direction | Strongest supporting evidence | Strongest challenge | Missing discriminating evidence | Maximum claim now |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Source matters under fixed authorization | Corrected 24-base matched source contrast and full threshold frontiers | Legitimate fact dependence can be expected; endpoint and joint contrasts differ | Externally distinctive operating interpretation and same-workflow effect bridge only if claimed | Scoped empirical instrument result |
| Reference agreement can coexist with forbidden effects | R156 original-source exact Qwen BEFORE and Gemma AFTER email admissions | Protected Qwen AFTER, adapted requests/task roots | Actual released-driver native hostile/neutral panel | Observed local explicit-contract counterexamples |
| One witness can release too many occurrences | One-reference fixed-actor native/reconstructed cases | Some autonomous exact failures have two reference witnesses | Same fixed candidate/reference and actual identified allocation; no hidden relation change | Specific admission-capacity behavior |
| Capacity alone restores authorization | Helps selected atomic duplicates | Excessive references, fresh sequential references, L3 and A17 reversals | None needed to reject universality; practical repair requires legal alternatives | Universal claim falsified |
| Continuous review prevents harmful retries | Source skip logic and two O96 autonomous Gemma payment retries | Reviewed excess occurs; restored review might approve | Authentic patched/inert full continuation and utility under same prefix | Coverage route observed; necessity/sufficiency unproved |
| Retained hostile text causes all reference errors | All D236 violating requests include hostile directions; some reconstructed edits change references | Protected references, direct/derived co-exposure, reconstruction effects | Actual-input reference-only contrast with facts/history preserved | Conditional hypothesis, not universal mechanism |
| Guard blocking uniquely causes unsafe recovery | Selected clean A10 and restrictive L3 continuations | Actual service interruption and older undefended retries | Existing selected counterexamples already defeat uniqueness; fresh held-out matched paths bound scope | Generic recovery remains viable |
| Default-key text changes do not alter the backend | Six paired future retry-continuation tests | Other APIs may assign different identities | Source/tool-specific future semantics if transferred | Finite authored-backend equivalence |
| Replay misses are authorization failures | Immediate and downstream fidelity disagreement | Native review: core-compatible variants, reads, promises/errors; nine unresolved entries | Independent adjudication of remaining ambiguous contracts if used | Fidelity/measurement evidence, not breach count |
| Gate fixes the problem | Three actual R156 denials; earlier containing workflows | Privileged contract/state; some tasks still incomplete | Fair runtime-information comparator and whole-task usefulness | Conventional containment positive control |
| The phenomenon generalizes | Payment and native email sinks; two model configurations | E5 zero violations/24; tasks and role/transport bundles confounded | New supported profile/structure predictions, failure and protection | Local boundaries, no population generality |
| Full published configuration was defeated | Core hash and source-original methods | Effective auxiliary inputs, parser/decoding, tasks and limits differ | Qualified selected release driver, named scope; principal-profile claim needs that actual profile | Adapted/source-component finding only |

There is no required universal causal explanation. A reference can be excessive, a lawful reference can be matched to a resource-changing action, review can be skipped, or conventional execution enforcement can contain all three. Use the operative path in each trace, not one post-hoc R/B/H classification.

### W.3 Rival hypotheses and genuinely different predictions

| Rival | Prediction in the current study or a justified follow-up | What would weaken it |
|---|---|---|
| H1: hostile reference generation | C1/C0 alters actual reference multiplicity/content at comparable exposed decisions; an original-reference exact route admits extra effects | Released C1 references protect; similar clean failures without the directive |
| H2: reusable correspondence | Fixed lawful one-occurrence reference admits two independent actor effects under source membership | Only excessive two-witness references fail; actual allocation contains the extra |
| H3: missing remaining-state enforcement | Fresh support can recur after a prior effect consumes the contract; G denies the extra | Reference consistently refuses after consumption; another native layer contains it |
| H4: contextual matching/representation | Equivalent backend arguments can change first admission; SOURCE/PAIR differ even with the same action set | Actual released driver or semantic-equivalence check eliminates the claimed contrast |
| H5: ordinary interruption recovery | Matched service and guard prefixes produce comparable continuation under the same effective inputs; accurate committed-state feedback can matter | A reproducible guard-specific divergence survives full input/route qualification |
| H6: study-profile artifact | Correct released schemas, decoding, parser, feedback or task scope changes the reachable failure/protection boundary | A declared supported release profile reproduces the same consequence |
| H7: conventional authorization suffices | G prevents effects using an ordinary explicit contract and ledger | Only if the gate is incomplete or policy semantics are wrong; successful G alone is not novelty |
| H8: ordinary task failure dominates | Clean and hostile twins share the failures; physical/reporting errors explain many labels | Hostile exposed twins show a distinct forbidden effect with clean/functionality controls intact |

These hypotheses can coexist. C1/C0 whole-workflow differences do not by themselves identify a reference mediator; source/reference edits require a valid fixed-state intervention. A positive G result does not prove every upstream cause is irrelevant. A protective outcome can expose a useful mechanism, while absence of carrier delivery tests no post-exposure defense.

### W.4 Formal objects that clarify, rather than decorate

Let C be a trusted contract, s the actual backend state, h a committed effect prefix, a a pending operation and E(s,a) its observed ordered effect sequence. A failed request may have E empty or nonempty; observation may also be unresolved. Define the prefix-closed safety language L(C) separately from physical goal Q, required protocol P and reporting Z.

\[
\operatorname{Permit}_C(s,h,a)=1
\quad\text{iff}\quad
h\mathbin{\|}E(s,a)\in L(C),
\]

when the actual effect semantics are resolved. Unknown effect is not the empty sequence. The guard's admission A(a,R,H) and the independent permission predicate are different objects; a generated reference R is model behavior under H, not a new grant.

**State aliasing:** if the complete operative input to a local decision is identical for two states with opposite permission labels, that decision cannot distinguish the labels from that input. This elementary observation locates an interface limitation. It does not prove the whole guard lacks state, that a model ignored visible facts or that an upstream generator/downstream gate cannot compensate. The CPU exact-rule certificate supplies a local witness, not a novel theorem.

**Contextual non-nesting:** source whole-set judgment J(a,{r1,r2}) need not equal the OR of singleton judgments J(a,{r1}) and J(a,{r2}). Compare PAIR-M0 and PAIR-M1 on the same identified compatibility graph to isolate capacity. SOURCE-to-PAIR changes the relation, so A17 cannot be interpreted as a pure monotone restriction. Even a genuine first-step restriction does not order later adaptive trajectories: different released prefixes and feedback produce new proposals and references.

**Counterfactual scope:** fixed-suffix replay estimates substitution under an imposed future; authenticated fresh continuation estimates a checkpoint-specific policy intervention; full autonomous policy arms estimate realized complete execution under different packages. None automatically estimates an internal representation, necessity, natural mediation effect or deployment incidence. Equal visible feedback is insufficient for full input equivalence. Shared parents reduce the number of independent structures.

These definitions generate tests: inspect complete inputs for opposite-state aliases; certify actual backend equivalence before argument normalization; hold relation edges fixed for a capacity contrast; match committed prefixes and effective requests for interruption comparisons; and score safety/physical/protocol/reporting separately. No general formal guarantee or source refinement proof is established.

### W.5 Cross-run invariants, reversals and neglected connections

1. **Behavioral agreement and effect legality repeatedly separate.** Early duplicate payments, later exact email support and clean over-total continuations differ mechanistically but share a need for prefix-relative permission. That is a unifying audit object, not proof of a new universal defect.
2. **Protection is structurally informative.** Original live ON protection, Qwen AFTER empty references, exhausted-task stopping and keyed retries prevent a story of indiscriminate guard failure. A useful explanation must predict these too.
3. **“Spent” is not one manipulation.** D236's explicit spent task differs in objective and hostile text; T22 matches more information but I/N still changes an initialization/history bundle; partial L3/A10 histories are different again. Pooling them as one consumption-state test would manufacture a contradiction.
4. **Local safety restrictions need not preserve task viability.** Fixed-edge L3 restriction blocks a lawful installment and precedes overshoot; SOURCE/PAIR A17 uses a different contextual relation; E5 group email under-completes. These are related usefulness obligations, not interchangeable proofs of repair harm.
5. **Success ambiguity predates the guard.** The undefended Slack retry after `None`, ordinary service interruption and error-after-commit records make receipt/state visibility a serious rival to security-specific feedback.
6. **Zero top-level errors conceal different events.** D236 has eighty tool errors; A182/R156 have reporting omissions; some R156 errors follow commits. The same episode summary cannot distinguish all three.
7. **Representation has tool-specific semantics.** Empty/default payment keys are equivalent on tested futures; principal case is materially different in native Slack. A blanket normalizer would erase the wrong distinctions.
8. **Transfer has already been tested and can fail.** E5's zero/24 must stay in the core evidence hierarchy. More repetitions or another family sharing the same wrapper would not erase that negative boundary.

### W.6 Historical-path and counterfactual-project tests

The original source-influence framing encouraged linking heterogeneous scores, routes and downstream outcomes. The first R/B implementation leads then encouraged classifying each new trace as continuity or multiplicity. Duplicate-payment results elevated reference contamination; the striking controlled 4+10 result elevated repair-induced recovery; authentic A10/A17 then shifted attention to representation-sensitive admission. Each shift was informative, but none should become a compulsory organizing narrative because code or terminology already exists.

The most consequential self-correction is the replay audit: a literal fidelity target was too easily treated as authority ground truth; fifteen review packets even used the wrong history. Correcting that construct changes what the old evidence can support without changing its frozen failed gate. This is an epistemic improvement, not a reason to discard the original method or relabel failures as successes.

**If all current data existed without historical plans**, the next paper would begin with an effects-based audit of a qualified released reference guard, reporting protection and counterexamples under matched trusted contracts. It would use old scoring/replay evidence only where it resolves a necessary construct or route. It would not begin by promising a generic history-laundering class, universal capacity repair or harmful guard feedback.

That reconstruction favors the current 36 matched workflows plus conditional pairs over another large payment or selected-checkpoint sweep. It also imposes a stop condition: if the faithful profile protects or standard interruption/enforcement explains everything, the strongest honest output may be a narrower measurement/implementation study rather than the desired S&P paper.

### W.7 Seven skeptical reviewers and concrete responses

| Reviewer | Strong objection | Required evidence/design response | If not achieved |
|---|---|---|---|
| Novelty skeptic | “Agreement is not access control; you rediscovered a missing budget.” | A consequential supported-profile distinction and exact nearest-work delta beyond counters, idempotency or complete mediation | Narrow contribution; do not invent terminology |
| Mechanism skeptic | “Contamination correlates with failure; PAIR also changed the predicate.” | Actual operative input/route, fixed-candidate reference intervention if needed, same edge graph for capacity | Conditional observation only |
| Rigor skeptic | “Small selected roots, overwritten snapshots, wrong annotation packets and hidden reporting metrics.” | Authenticated histories, immutable proposal IDs, independent effect observer, all scheduled rows and orthogonal outcomes | Exclude invalid inference, preserve raw failures |
| Profile skeptic | “You changed schemas, decoding, parser, feedback and limits.” | Actual release-driver capture/inert parity, named model-role profile and benchmark scope | Adapted claim only |
| Generalization skeptic | “Two families share a wrapper; six roots are not a population.” | Exact supported scope, fresh structural predictions, a second qualified profile only when needed | No architecture-wide or prevalence claim |
| Threat-model skeptic | “Clean A10 is not prompt injection.” | Matched C0/C1, genuine carrier delivery before autonomous harmful proposal, trusted permission fixed | Clean reliability/authorization result only |
| Scientific-understanding skeptic | “A list of bugs does not explain anything new.” | One predictive condition that explains a failure and a protected neighbor, strongest rival confronted | Separate findings or stop broad paper framing |

The reviewers need not agree: one may value a serious scoped bug while another demands a broader mechanism. The design does not promise to satisfy them by increasing file or episode counts. The claim must answer the objection it actually makes relevant.

### W.8 Lessons from paper analysis and nearest-work discipline

The detailed full/targeted reading registers in Appendices D/G/L/R are inherited documentary records. Their useful methodological standards are: define a concrete protected object; make each experimental component answer a named ambiguity; test the strongest simple rival; separate capability, delivered exposure, admission and consequence; retain negative/protective controls; validate common interfaces against native behavior; and state observer/trust assumptions and legitimate-use costs. Accepted status does not prove a paper's methods correct or prescribe an acceptance recipe.

AttriGuard's published per-call counterfactual-survival purpose makes an effect-level boundary directly relevant, but task-aligned injection, contextual alternatives and copied history are already part of its setting. ToolSafe constrains a generic claim about security feedback and continued reasoning. ACE/DRIFT and stateful authorization/receipt work constrain “check remaining permission” as novelty. Permissions research separates prediction from enforcement and coverage from accuracy. No new complete-paper or artifact comparison of every nearest method was performed for this reconciliation.

The other supplied report checked AttriGuard/ToolSafe/permissions bibliographic records and explicitly did not claim an exhaustive novelty audit. Its proposed DataSentinel comparison is a lead, not a completed implementation evaluation. Preserve that scope. Likewise, the long catalog of authority/memory/preprint leads below does not establish that every title or publication-status assertion has been independently verified. Reverify primary methods and artifacts for whichever final claim survives; do not cite an inherited name as proof of a novel gap.

### W.9 Revised claim hierarchy and contribution structure

| Level | Permissible current claim | What is needed to advance |
|---|---|---|
| Primitive observation | Explicit simulated contracts were exceeded in recorded adapted runs; selected nearby cases protected | Stable source/state/request/effect joins, already supplied at stated scopes |
| Local empirical regularity | Specific matching, reference and retry structures recur in these development cohorts | Preserve shared ancestry, task/profile limits and contradictory cells |
| Conditional causal result | Authenticated selected admission interventions alter the first prefix and fresh continuation | Name changed relation/feedback; do not generalize to all tasks or attacker causation |
| Mechanistic interpretation | Particular reference or routing dependence explains a selected path | Rival-specific intervention and full operative inputs |
| Supported release-profile boundary | A real selected release driver admits or contains a forbidden effect on new matched native roots | Current Section 7 qualification and actual results |
| Predictive transferable contribution | One condition predicts both failure and protection beyond an adapted task | Fresh structural/profile tests and nearest-work distinction |
| General theorem, prevalence or universal defense | Not established | Different formal, sampling or implementation evidence; not promised here |

Candidate paper contributions, conditional on results, are: (1) a validated effect-grounded audit method and construct corrections; (2) a consequential release-executed boundary with matched protection and attacker controls; (3) discriminating diagnosis of reference/admission/state versus ordinary interruption; and (4) a safety/functionality tradeoff against a conventional trusted gate. Do not preallocate all four or require a new defense. Original author-owned contributions may supply any valid part of the final argument.

### W.10 What to abandon, freeze and do next

**Abandon as current claims:** literal miss equals breach; downstream score equals autonomous safe recovery; universal contamination; universal capacity safety; globally stricter SOURCE-to-PAIR; uniquely security-induced overshoot; local model support being inherently invalid; source hash proving whole-profile identity; a six-root panel estimating prevalence; and an acceptance prediction. Retire the 174-slot active queue and automatic 250-entry rerun.

**Freeze and preserve:** original unpublished manuscript/reviews; corrected source contrasts and complete threshold frontiers; protective original live results; all raw completed/failed/partial cohorts; exact contracts, model/source identities and effective requests; original/transformed review packets with the error documented; nine semantic unknowns; frozen suffix results with their actual estimand; A10/A17, L3 and E5 contrary evidence; existing code/test artifacts and unexecuted manifests. No correction overwrites sole raw data or claims independent human review.

**Priority order:** release-driver CPU parity and native observer qualification; finalize six roots and matched carrier contracts; implement/freeze/execute the 36 autonomous workflows; execute only eligible authentic interruption pairs within twelve slots; analyze per-root safety, physical/protocol/reporting, exposure and routes; choose one needed mechanism follow-up or narrow/stop. A later Gemma profile or independent confirmation is separately justified and budgeted. No historical ambiguous annotation blocks new explicit-contract work unless that claim relies on it.

Evidence required before a strong S&P-level claim is defensible: real supported implementation consequence; fixed trusted permission and genuine attacker reachability for exploit claims; independent effect/contract validity; the strongest alternative controlled; nontrivial prediction of protection and failure; honest structural/model scope; matched lawful functionality; exact novelty delta; and reproducibility from standalone artifacts. These are scientific obligations, not a numeric acceptance gate.

Remaining failure risks are substantial: the real released driver may protect; only an adapted profile may fail; ordinary recovery or conventional enforcement may fully explain the result; new tasks may never expose the mechanism; six roots may be too narrow; ambiguous semantic labels or schema support may limit claims; and close prior work may already establish the substantive distinction. A truthful narrower result is preferable to another broad run designed to preserve the narrative.

### W.11 Source map for this authority

| Source | Role | Evidence status |
|---|---|---|
| Original unpublished manuscript/reviews and retained author studies | Ownership, original contributions, corrected contrasts and reviewer concerns | Historical author-owned evidence, not external prior work |
| D60/D236/T21/T22/O54/O96/A182/R156 raw archives and frozen protocols | Completed scientific records, failures, controlled and autonomous effects | Different cohorts; never pooled as independent replications |
| `IEEE_SP_Global_Evidence_Audit_2026-10-24.md` and kit | Primitive audit, cross-project falsification, historical path and reviewer analysis | Completed analysis of retained records |
| `IEEE_SP_Semantic_and_Profile_Verdict_2026-10-24.md` and evidence kit | Authentic contexts, 110 native replays, 390 mappings, source/profile certificates | Completed offline single-analyst review; no new model generation |
| `IEEE_SP_Detailed_Scientific_Report_and_Release_Profile_Study_2026-10-24(1).md` | Independent synthesis criticism, matched-carrier allocation, release-driver qualification and reviewer stress tests | Supplied secondary analysis/design, not a new replication or full-literature audit |
| Actual retained public release, drivers and runtime | Source identity, request construction, exact/fuzzy/skip logic and native effects | CPU/source evidence; complete new live profile qualification remains prospective |
| Sections 7 and 13.4 / Appendices P/T | Active native protocol versus inactive/conditional controls | Prospective methods; no new execution asserted |

The supplied detailed report's strongest design correction is adopted: a 48-slot ceiling cannot simultaneously supply twelve independent roots, clean/hostile twins, three complete policies and interruption controls. The authority uses six roots, twelve carrier instances, 36 full workflows and at most twelve shared-parent continuations. Its proposed interruption comparison is further qualified here with explicit eligibility, real service-fault execution, complete input matching and common-feedback scope. Its evidence summaries do not become fresh independent replications by incorporation.
